#2858. Eagles-Giants: Philadelphia wastes six sacks in seventh straight loss

The Eagles found a pass rush Sunday but never found enough offense to make it count. Philadelphia lost 19-11 to the New York Giants at Lincoln Financial Field, extending its losing streak to seven games and entering Week 10 on a 2-7 record.

Joe Milton's first Eagles start showed flashes of athleticism but no sustained offensive turnaround. He passed for 169 yards, rushed for 64 and converted a two-point attempt, but Philadelphia surrendered seven sacks and scored only three points after the first quarter. New York improved to 5-2-1.

The Giants opened with field goals from 53 and 49 yards before Philadelphia answered with its only touchdown drive. Saquon Barkley scored from 3 yards out, and Milton's two-point conversion gave the Eagles an 8-6 advantage.

Philadelphia carried that lead into halftime behind five sacks and a defense that repeatedly disrupted New York's possessions. The offense, however, could not create separation, leaving the game available for a second-half swing.

Jameis Winston supplied that swing after replacing J.J. McCarthy, who left with a hip injury before later returning. Winston completed 6 of 8 passes for 71 yards and capped a 14-play, 76-yard possession with a 5-yard touchdown pass to Isaiah Likely.

The touchdown put New York ahead 13-8 with 2:25 remaining in the third quarter. Philadelphia never regained the lead.

The Eagles finished with 220 total yards, converted five of 18 third-down opportunities, and went one-for-three on fourth down. Milton lost a fumble, while the running backs combined for only 28 rushing yards on 16 carries.

Dominic Zvada's two fourth-quarter field goals extended New York's advantage to 19-8. Jake Elliott's 45-yard field goal cut the deficit to eight, but Philadelphia ended its final possession on downs.

Zack Baun recorded two sacks and three tackles for loss before a back injury cut his afternoon short. Jeremiah Trotter Jr. led Philadelphia with 11 tackles, and Jalen Carter, Jordan Davis, Jonathan Greenard and Nolan Smith each added a sack.

Makai Lemon led the Eagles with six receptions for 61 yards. Dallas Goedert caught five passes for 50 yards.

Philadelphia enters its bye needing more than a quarterback adjustment. Protection, rushing production, and sustained offense remain unresolved, while Baun's health becomes another important storyline. A defense that kept the game close could not compensate for an offense that produced one touchdown.

#2857. NFL’s Madrid Game Suffers ‘Embarrassing’ Lights-Out Moment Mid-Play

Joe Burrow and the Cincinnati Bengals were closing in on a win in Madrid when the stadium suddenly stole the spotlight by losing it. The lights at Real Madrid's Santiago Bernabéu briefly went out during a fourth-quarter play against the Atlanta Falcons, leaving players in near-darkness and some viewers wondering whether their televisions were acting up. The interruption lasted only seconds, but it forced a replay of the down and gave fans an unexpected talking point from Cincinnati's 34-17 victory.

With Cincinnati comfortably ahead late in the game, Burrow handed the ball to running back Samaje Perine. Then, mid-play, the stadium lighting abruptly cut out.

Perine appeared to gain enough yards for a first down and continued his run as most of the field went dark. A few smaller lights remained visible before the main lighting quickly returned.

Officials wiped out the play and had the Bengals replay the down. Cincinnati still got the first down, keeping the brief interruption from changing the outcome.

The Bengals still left Spain with the win. Meanwhile, the stadium gave viewers a moment that had nothing to do with Burrow's passing.

🚨🚨THIS IS WILD🚨🚨The stadium lights went OUT IN THE MIDDLE OF A PLAY during today's #Bengals vs. #Falcons game in Madrid.😳😳😳The players were already running a play when the lights suddenly went out.HAVE NEVER SEEN ANYTHING LIKE THIS… pic.twitter.com/XrOx1EAUow

— MLFootball (@MLFootball) November 8, 2026

The sudden darkness prompted confusion online, with viewers trying to figure out whether the problem was happening in Madrid or in their living rooms. "The lights go embarrassingly out at the Bernabeu stadium towards the end of this Bengals-Falcons NFL game in Madrid. Oh dear," one user wrote.

Another said the interruption initially looked like a television problem. "I thought my TV was messing up, but no, the lights went out in the Madrid Stadium in the #Bengals vs #Falcons game. #NFL," they posted.

A third took aim at the league's international operation, writing, "'The down will be replayed because the lights went out' – Madrid game @NFL international is a joke."

The glitch was resolved quickly, but those few seconds were enough to pull attention away from the action on the field.

The Seahawks' social media admin risked Swiftie wrath, earning praise from fans who called them a legend.

Jay-Z's Super Bowl reveal took a twist following his "selfish" admission.

Jalen Hurts' loyalty to the Eagles came under scrutiny amid "unfounded" injury claims.

As his NFL career takes a downturn, Hurts' biggest victory has come away from the football field.

Taylor Swift appeared to snap at an NFL fan as security stepped in.

Jason Pohuski/Cal Sport Media Newscom/MEGA

Before the stadium went dark, Burrow had already made the afternoon difficult for Atlanta. The Bengals quarterback completed 24 of 29 passes for three touchdowns, with Ja'Marr Chase and Tee Higgins giving the Falcons plenty to chase.

Chase caught 10 passes for 155 yards and a touchdown, doing much of his damage before halftime. His performance came with an awkward draft reminder for Atlanta: The Falcons selected tight end Kyle Pitts fourth overall in 2021, just one pick before Cincinnati grabbed Chase.

Higgins delivered two touchdown catches, including a crucial eight-yard score after Atlanta pulled within seven points in the third quarter. That pushed Cincinnati's advantage back to 14 and helped put the comeback out of reach.

The win extended the Bengals' winning streak to four games and improved their record to 6-2, tying Pittsburgh atop the AFC North.

Atlanta had promising moments, especially from running back Bijan Robinson, but couldn't keep pace with Cincinnati for long. The Falcons cut the deficit to 24-17 in the third quarter before another Burrow touchdown pass to Higgins restored the Bengals' two-score advantage.

A missed field goal made the afternoon even more frustrating. Nick Folk sent a 38-yard attempt wide left, ending his streak of 107 consecutive successful field goals from 40 yards or closer. It was another missed opportunity for an Atlanta team trying to stay within reach.

The loss dropped the Falcons to 3-6, while Cincinnati finished with a convincing 17-point victory.

Even an unexpected lights-out moment could only briefly interrupt the Bengals' finish, to the excitement of their fans.

I think the batters have to manage our skills and preparation well. I know we have a very good batting line-up with some very senior players... Dinesh Chandimal, Dhananjaya de Silva, myself, and a couple of other guys."Everyone knows how to play on a green wicket. We played really well in England also, so I have no doubt in my batting line-up," Mendis told reporters.

The Rawalpindi Test will mark Mendis' first assignment as Sri Lanka's Test captain after he took over the leadership from Dhananjaya de Silva in early October. The 31-year-old admitted that leading the side in the longest format was something he was particularly looking forward to, despite the challenges of an extended stay in Pakistan.Sri Lanka have spent more than a month in the country, having arrived for the preceding white-ball assignments. Mendis acknowledged that spending such a long period away from home, largely between hotels and training sessions, had not been easy for the players.However, he said the squad had found ways to keep itself occupied and was now fully focused on switching gears for Test cricket."Honestly, compared to ODIs and T20s, Test cricket is very different. I think everyone mostly likes to play Test cricket. Me as well. I'm very happy to captain in Test cricket," he said."It's not easy, sometimes we stay in the hotel and come to practice and that's it... I think it's a little bit hard to stay one month in a hotel. But we do some stuff in the team room, games and individual things to keep ourselves entertained."

Sri Lanka are yet to register a Test victory in their two series this year against West Indies and India, but Mendis believes the performances of his bowling unit offer plenty of encouragement ahead of the Pakistan challenge.The skipper pointed to the experience within the attack, highlighting how the fast bowlers have delivered consistently across formats and are equipped to handle the demands of Test cricket.Sri Lanka have several pace options at their disposal, with Asitha Fernando, Lahiru Kumara, Dilshan Madushanka and Eshan Malinga forming the fast-bowling contingent alongside all-rounder Milan Rathnayake. Left-arm spinner Prabath Jayasuriya will lead the spin department."We have a very balanced side, both with the bowling and batting unit. I think we've done really well in the past couple of tours in the bowling side, and same in one-day cricket. The fast-bowling attack is doing well," Mendis said."We have a very senior bowling unit, the guys have played so many first-class and international matches. Same in the batting, so everyone is ready for tomorrow's game."

Beyond preparations for the series, Mendis also expressed satisfaction with the arrangements made by the Pakistan Cricket Board during Sri Lanka's extended stay in the country.The Sri Lankan captain praised the security arrangements and said the players had been comfortable with the local food, which he felt was similar to the cuisine back home."Security-wise, everybody is very happy. Food, we normally eat spicy and everything, so same like in Sri Lanka it's in Pakistan. I think everyone has had a happy stay in Pakistan," he added.

#2853. The AI ‘credit card’ is getting costlier as CoreWeave bond yield tops 16%

Debt markets are sending a message to AI data center builders: we're hiking the interest rate on your credit card and may put some new curbs on your credit limit.

That analogy comes courtesy of Jefferies global head of equity strategy, Christopher Wood, who put it in a note on Friday: "The most plausible way the AI capex arms race will end is that the credit card will be taken away."

That isn't happening yet, but credit markets have been spooked by the new political landscape — seeing more hurdles to data centers under Democratic governors in key states, along with a Democratic-controlled Congress.

The poster child for debt stress is neocloud Coreweave. The yield on its 2032 bond with a 9.625% coupon has climbed above 16%. Coreweave's five-year credit default swaps spiked above 1,289 basis points on Thursday, widening above their October peak, according to Deutsche Bank. The CDS eased 8% Friday to around 1,155.

"We've had plenty of warnings from the credit space," said Jim Reid, Deutsche Bank's global head of macro and thematic research, in a note on Friday. Debt and equities are now moving more in sync, he added, "so we'll have to see if credit goes any further."

Investors are demanding more compensation to insure against default among even companies with the strongest balance sheets and credit ratings, including Nvidia, Broadcom, and Amazon.com.

CDS on Nvidia debt is up 76% in the last month. Broadcom's CDS is up 49%, while Amazon's has gained 44%. Their CDS remain low on an absolute basis; Nvidia's spread stood at roughly 139 basis points, compared with about 298 bps for Oracle and roughly 1,100 for CoreWeave. A large increase from a low starting point does not imply the same credit risk.

Still, rising CDS are a sign that markets want more protection against default among even the largest and healthiest debt issuers. And rising CDS imply higher financing costs down the line.

The market is now grappling with the prospect that at least some data center spending will be delayed or scrapped under incoming Democratic governors in Ohio, Texas, and Georgia, the three states with much of the planned capacity.

Moratoriums could impact 45% of planned capacity in the U.S. with a large amount of that in Texas, according to Goldman Sachs. Texas governor-elect Gina Hinojosa has said she's not opposed to data centers but wants a moratorium until the state legislature passes protections for communities.

None of these means AI debt financing is collapsing. Goldman tallies five AI bond deals totaling $67 billion since October 29, pushing total hyperscaler debt issuance this year to $296 billion. Goldman forecasts $440 billion of hyperscaler debt issuance next year, plus an additional $300 billion of project-level issuance across investment-grade and high-yield.

The Trump administration is vowing to support data centers. National Economic Council Director Kevin Hassett told CNBC on Friday that he expects data center growth to continue as forecast, despite Democrats' big election wins.

The big question now is how quickly AI spending will generate enough cash for hyperscalers to keep investors comfortable with future capex plans. For the neoclouds, any sign of consolidation or retrenchment in capex could result in fewer or shorter contracts for data center compute or delays in prepayments.

Goldman analysts said hyperscalers collectively reported negative free cash flow in the third quarter as capital spending exceeded operating cash flow.

"Delayed monetization and growth in purchase commitments—which we view as additional and sizable uses of cash, separate from Capex—are the key risks to more debt issuance, subject to market conditions," according to the bank.

Peak AI debt issuance may now be closer at hand. Bank of America analysts estimates that AI-related credit issuance will rise 31% from 2026 to 2027, hitting $685 billion, including public and private debt. They then see it falling 23% to $531 billion in 2028.

As for the risk? "Downside to these forecasts exists from a potential slowdown in AI infrastructure projects on the back of the Blue Wave, which will reduce capex needs and thus debt market funding," BofA said in a note.

-CNBC's Nick Wells and Michael Bloom contributed reporting

Note: "Losers" is not a judgment on the talent or character of any individual. It is simply a categorization of the play in one game – no disrespect is intended or should be inferred

The Dolphins' punter has struggled this year, especially in terms of consistency. Also serving as the kickoff specialist, Pinion was flagged on the opening kickoff for failing to get the ball into the landing zone. In the second quarter, he shanked a punt for just 25 yards. It was not a banner day for the Dolphins' punter. Pinion did have a 46-yard pass to running back Kaytron Allen on a fake punt, which nearly pushed him off this list, but the season-long inconsistency keeps him here.

So many flags were thrown against the Dolphins this week, especially against the offensive line. Counting declined penalties, Miami was flagged 12 times in the game. It feels more like a rookies trying too hard more than an issue of players not paying attention.

Rookie receiver Chris Bell was just non-existent on Sunday. He was targeted one time and recorded no receptions. While Douglas continues to break out, Bell is struggling to produce. The potential is still there, and he will break out at some point, but right now, he is a complete afterthought for the offense.

Lions head coach Dan Campbell's first coaching job was with the Dolphins, starting as an intern, then as the tight ends coach, and ending as the team's interim head coach after Joe Philbin was fired. Campbell showed flashes of what he would become as a head coach, but he was not yet ready to get the job full time. There was definitely temptation there.

Campbell instead went to the New Orleans Saints as their tight ends and assistant head coach. Campbell moved to the Lions in 2021 and has worked therm through a rebuild into a team that should be a contender for a deep run in the NFC playoffs. After Campbell's interim stint, Miami hired Adam Gase, then Brian Flores, then Mike McDaniel, and now Jeff Hafley.

The Lions coaching staff also includes defensive coordinator Kelvin Sheppard, defensive line coach Kacy Rodgers, and outside linebackers coach David Carrao – all of whom were Dolphins players or coaches.

Willis is clearly bothered by the groin injury that has been on the injury report for the last couple of weeks. He just doesn't have the same mobility we have seen from him before. He tried a couple of times to run away from pressure, but he just didn't have the speed to get going. He showed solid understanding of the situation and threw the ball away at times, but the lack of scrambling ability was very noticeable in the game.

Pinion's struggles (and the fake punt) were mentioned above, but overall, the Dolphins' special teams still aren't special. They gave up a fake punt, Zane Gonzalez blasted the upright on a field goal attempt to end the half, and overall, it feels like Miami cannot trust their special teams this year.

Tyler Kolek (13) looks to make a play during the Knicks' 109-97 win over the Cavaliers on Nov. 7, 2026 at Rocket Arena in Cleveland. NBAE via Getty Images

#2848. Lane Kiffin falls to 0-6 against Alabama despite pink locker room trick

The Alabama Crimson Tide dressed in Lane Kiffin's pink visiting locker room at Tiger Stadium on Saturday and walked out with a 31-13 win over the LSU Tigers. No visitor had won in Baton Rouge this season, and Alabama became the first to win after using the room Kiffin had painted this fall.

Kiffin took the idea from Iowa, where Hayden Fry painted the Kinnick Stadium visitors' quarters pink after arriving in 1979, and he used it at Ole Miss before coming to LSU. He explained the choice at his Oct. 12 news conference, which 247Sports transcribed.

"We did that at the last place, and I think we did it about two years ago. We never lost a home game. We haven't lost a home game yet. Maybe it works. That's why," Kiffin said.

Oct 24, 2026; Auburn, Alabama, USA; LSU Tigers head coach Lane Kiffin looks on during the second quarter against the Auburn Tigers at Jordan-Hare Stadium. Mandatory Credit: John Reed-Imagn Images

LSU outgained Alabama 457-285 and led with 16 minutes to play. It lost by 18 because it went 0-for-4 on fourth down, missed a field goal and turned the ball over in Alabama territory, and Alabama turned those empty trips into a 14-0 fourth quarter.

The most debated call came with eight minutes left, when Kiffin kept his offense on the field on fourth-and-10 from the LSU 25.

"And then at the end of the game, you can play to just keep the score low when you're on your own 20, but you have no chance to win by punting really, analytically, so you go for it," Kiffin said.

Alabama's version of the afternoon centered on those same stops. Keelon Russell played through an ankle injury and threw three touchdown passes, and Kalen DeBoer opened his postgame news conference with the defense, in remarks On3 transcribed.

"Just super proud of the guys. Team win all around. Defense coming up with some big stops. I believe they were 0-for-4 on fourth down and got us some field position, and offense taking advantage of that when we did," DeBoer said.

Alabama Crimson Tide head coach Kalen Deboer talks on the sideline Thursday, Jan. 1, 2026, during the Rose Bowl and quarterfinal game of the College Football Playoff against Indiana Hoosiers at Rose Bowl Stadium in Pasadena, Calif.

Alabama has now won seven of its last eight games at Tiger Stadium, including a 42-13 rout there in 2024 before any of the walls changed color. The paint gave Kiffin a talking point and his visitors a strange pregame setting, and Saturday showed how little either one counts once a team comes up empty on four fourth downs.

The loss dropped LSU to 6-3 and 5-1 at home, and its playoff hopes now depend on help from elsewhere with the Texas Longhorns coming to Baton Rouge next. Kiffin is now 0-6 against Alabama as a head coach, across his stops at Tennessee, Ole Miss and LSU.

#2847. CeeDee Lamb’s fumble vs. the Colts is the most chaotic play of the NFL season

The 2026 NFL campaign is just reaching the midway point of the regular season.

But you might not find a wilder play than this fumble by Dallas Cowboys wide receiver CeeDee Lamb Sunday against the Indianapolis Colts.

With the visiting Cowboys facing a 3rd-and-12 in their own territory, while holding a 20-17 lead, Indianapolis brought the blitz against quarterback Dak Prescott. With several pass rushers in his face, Prescott got off a throw to Lamb along the right sideline. Because Prescott was unable to step into the throw, the pass was underthrown and Lamb had to break back to the football.

Which he did, making a diving catch in front of rookie cornerback Kenneth Harris.

And that is exactly when the chaos began.

Harris slipped on his own break, and that allowed Lamb enough separation to make the diving catch. But Harris never touchdown Lamb down, who alertly got to his feet and raced upfield, picking up a block as he sliced into the red zone.

That's when he was tackled, lost the ball, and following a challenge from Colts head coach Shane Steichen, Indianapolis took over possession.

The FOX Sports booth speculated that because he is a rookie, Harris believed in the moment that Lamb did not need to be touched down. After all, in the college game once a player goes to the ground with the football, they are down at that spot and the play is whistled dead.

Instead, he gave Lamb a chance to pick up additional yardage, only for the Dallas WR to fumble the ball away after what was, for a brief moment, a brilliant play.

And no one was happier for that fumble than Harris.

#2845. Game Recap: Suns’ defensive intensity proves decisive in 102-99 win against Rockets

Rick Flair drip go "Woo!" on a(n)… all- time great.

The Suns dig deep to pull out a 102-99 victory over the Houston Rockets to win the KD trade for the first time this year.

What a wonderful game. One that gives us a blueprint for how the Suns are going to make noise in the perennially competitive Western Conference. This was a statement win and the best the Suns have tallied far, early in the season.

After tonight, I see a real path forward for the Suns to meet Ishbia's lofty goals of a second-round playoff appearance. In a game that could have been derailed by social media distractions, the Suns dug deep after a horrific 22-3 run to close the half to pull out a clutch-time victory against, possibly, the best team in the Western Conference. How did the Suns right the ship and pull out a competitive win against a team that swept the season series last year? Energy. The Phoenix Suns season is officially a question of energy.

So much has been made about fit but after tonight, it becomes irrelevant. It's not about the points, or the rebounds, or even the minutes a player delivers for the Phoenix Suns that will determine the wins and losses. It's the energy they can provide. Energy that leads to turnovers under the basket after a miss. Energy that leads to locking down defensive rebounds that lead to transition opportunities for the plethora of scorers on the Suns roster. Energy from blocks that lead to points, or drawing offensive fouls that take points away. The Suns rode the energy in the first quarter only to be hit by a brown out in the second, and in the end they found a way to generate enough power to match the Rockets' energy plays blow for blow through a contested third and outlasting a desperate Sengun (who doesn't want it with KhOso) looking to salvage a forgettable night with a fourth-quarter push that was too little too late.

I don't know if there is any player I can target as standing out in game that needed a whole team to secure a win. The Suns had 5 players finish the night in double figures, including a surgical Luke Kennard who proved to be the a fusion of Royce and Grayson Allen in a flurry of points in limited minutes. Booker put up 24 points on 24 shots, and Jalen Green went 50% from the field on 7-of-14 shooting, with 6 assists. The decision-making is coming along. The Villain, under the spotlight after his online spat with KD, held the former Sun to limited shots in the first half, but couldn't keep him off the line, where he grabbed 10 points on 11 attempts and ended the night with a hollow 21 points.

KhOso tag-teamed Sengun all night, who remained quiet for 3 and 1/2 quarters before making a push in the final six minutes. He finished the night with 15 points on 5/16 shooting with a modest 6 rebounds and a -16 plus-minus. If you're looking for a reason the Suns won the game, look no further than Sengun's 1-7 shooting with 4 minutes left in the third quarter. While there were plenty of mistakes from the young center duo, KhOso took advantage of some key injuries to Capella and Adams, and did enough inside to give the Suns the opportunity to win the game tonight.

It was the best of times; it was the worst of times.

The Suns got Jalen Green going early, starting 4-of-4 in the opening minutes of the game. I believe this needs to be a focus, and I was happy to see Jalen experience some success early against his former team. I no longer want to hear that Jalen Green can't play next to Devin Booker, as he consistently made the right decision and his paint touches became a real difference-maker for stretches in this game.

The Suns continued their stretch of hot starts, finding the energy early in a run sparked by Oso pulling the chair on Sengun, leading to a turnover and lay-up for Jalen Green. An offensive goal-tend led to an Oso "jumper", then Dillon bodied Durant into a bad pass, leading to a scramble with Dunn causing and then creating a turnover, which led to a Dillon three-pointer and a Houston timeout with the Suns leading 17-11.

The Suns continue to press, and Brooks draws an offensive foul, then hits an iso turnaround. Green makes a steal that leads to a corner three from Ryan Dunn, who had himself another night, and Kennard opens up the last three minutes, by splitting the black see opening up an assist to Ryan Dunn. Brooks hits a bucket, and Khaman shows promising hands as he collects a pass off a cut to the basket for a dunk. Kennard hits a jumper and a three to end the first quarter with the Suns up 35-23.

In the Second quarter, the Suns continued to cook, scoring an early six points before the system glitched and a scoring brownout, which lasted the length of the second quarter, led to a 41-23 lead for the Suns, turning into a tied score of 47-47 going into halftime. Is what happened.

A string of negative energy plays compounded into outright disaster, sparked off by four straight turnovers from the Suns team and caused by a series of quick shots, poor decisions, and offensive rebounds that snowballed into a 22-3 run going into halftime. For so many teams, this could have been the end, the beginning of the end, but the Suns managed to secure some extra juice in a game that was clearly important to the two former Houston Rockets who are looking to prove their value against the team that recently cast them aside.

The third quarter opens up with a previously quiet Durant hitting a three-point bucket that is immediately answered by the Phoenix Suns star Devin Booker with a three-point bucket of his own. This is what I loved about this third quarter, where every Houston push was answered in kind by the Phoenix Suns.

The Suns' path to victory is their ability to bring more energy than the opposing team. It was clear as day in the first quarter when the Suns made all the plays and shots they needed to push to an early double-digit lead, and unfortunately even more clear in a starkly different second half where the Houston Rockets woke up and matched the Suns' energy, systematically dismantling the gains the Suns had made. Too often this has led to a power outage from the Phoenix Suns, but not tonight.

A miscommunication on a switch between Booker and Bridges gives up an open three. Open three-point shots lead to the Rockets taking a 57-53 lead with 8 minutes left in the third.

Then Brooks contests a KD drive, which leads to Miles Bridges getting to the free-throw line for two shots. Positive energy. Then Brooks fouls KD on a three-point shot, bad energy, which pushes the Rockets' lead to 61-55.

Brooks and Dunn force a steal after a missed foul shot. Energy. Khaman knocks a ball free, and Booker dives to the floor, leading to a jump ball where the Rockets are called for a foul. Energy. The Suns collect the rebound on a Sengun miss, which turns into a Jalen Green dunk. Energy.

A Dillon Brooks hits a three, and a Durant miss leads to Booker heading to the foul line to regain the lead at 66-65. Energy. Kevin Durant hits a three, which is answered with a three from Devin Booker, with 2:25 left in the quarter. Energy. Dunn grabs an offensive rebound that led to a Collin Gillespie three-pointer. Energy. Durant gets an offensive rebound, and the Rockets are up 73-72. Negative energy.

Booker answers with a back door cut that leads to an easy bucket and the Suns regaining the lead 74-73.

Booker gets out in transition and earns a trip to the free-throw line. The Rockets win a challenge, and the Suns give up a straight drive to the basket to Fred, but Collin immediately answers with his own drive to the hoop. Energy. The Suns finish the third quarter up 76-75.

The fourth quarter opens up with Jalen Green attacking the paint. An early drive leads to a ManMan offensive rebound and putback. Khaman takes a trip to the free-throw line, and JG follows up with a jumper to push the Suns' lead to 80-75 . The Suns give up an offensive rebound to Smith, and Gillespie takes a bad shot, which leads to a Sheppard three and the Rockets tie the game up at 82-82.

Jalen Green contests a rebound that deflects out of bounds, and the Suns win a challenge to keep the ball on their end. A JG paint touch leads to a Malauch offensive rebound and one opportunity. Another Green paint touch leads to Bridges getting a mismatch and scoring in isolation.

Sengun and Miles Bridges exchange buckets. Sengun hits a three, but Dillon the Villain answers with a three of his own. Energy. Green gets into the paint and finds his way to the free-throw line where he hits 1-2 to give the Suns a 93-91 advantage.

Oso attempts an entry pass to a cutting Green, which gets fumbled out of bounds. Sengun takes it to the hoop to tie it up at 93-93. A Durant miss leads to a Booker middy, but Sengun fights his way to the foul line, hitting two foul shots to tie it back up at 95-95.

After a Durant miss, the Suns find Booker for an open three off an inbound, which he hits to take a 100-95 lead with 3 minutes left in the game. KD misses a shot on a Brooks contest, and Booker gets to the hoop for a lay-up to push the lead to 102-95. (Headband Book 4 L)

The Rockets bring it back to 102-97 when they turn it over on an attempted entry pass to a Sengun with 1:32 seconds left. Booker misses two straight shots, and Durant hits a jumper with 45 seconds left to bring the score to 102-99. Booker and Green work the two-man game, which leads to an out-of-bounds play and an in-and-out three from Booker with 3.2 seconds on the shot clock.

The Rockets have the ball within one score of tying it up, but the shot misses, and Oso collects the Rebound to secure a Suns' win in a season-altering momentum game.

The Phoenix Suns take on the San Antonio Spurs and their otherworldly seven-footer coming off a 50-point performance. Definitely an outsized task for a Suns team just starting to find their rhythm. Can the Suns continue to stack energy plays that lead to runs and secure a second straight win over a Western Conference contender? I hope so. Now that they've finally found their footing, they can't afford to slide back.

#2840. Mikel Brown’s healthy but knows ‘it’s all about getting stronger’ for Nets

ATLANTA — Understand that "back" is the worst four-letter word for Nets fans.

They read about back woes, minutes limits and injury management, but Ben Simmons is what they see. And what they fear.

But with Mikel Brown Jr., they don't have to be afraid.

Despite the kid gloves the Nets have treated their rookie with, Brown's ankle is fine. And more importantly, so is his back.

Mikel Brown Jr., who scored 18 points, drives to the basket during the Nets' 124-121 loss to the Hawks on Nov. 7, 2026 in Atlanta. NBAE via Getty Images

While Brown was limited to 21 games for Louisville last season, sources told The Post that his back was structurally fine. That injury had just been a soft tissue one and is long since in the past. His current minutes limit is about the future and building strength for that — for who the Nets see as an eventual All-Star.

"It's all about getting stronger," Brown told The Post before scoring 18 points in the Nets' 124-121 loss to the Hawks on Saturday night. "This is my first year playing against this type of high level of athletes. You've got to be at an elite performance level body-wise. But that's just the everyday process when it comes to that stuff.

"I'm constantly in the weight room every day just strengthening the parts of my body I need to strengthen. But yeah, that's us going along with the plan. I'm all aboard with it. As long as we're winning games, that's all I care about."

With Brown being the reward for a long tanking season, fans are not only invested in his progress but still scarred from the Simmons experience — trading James Harden for what turned out to be just 90 ineffective games in three seasons.

"Nah, man, that was never the case," said Brown, who will not play Sunday in Dallas because he's not cleared for back-to-backs. "I've just been leaning on my performance staff. My body feels great. Just take it slow. It's an 82-game season. We want to be in a position to make the playoffs, so every game matters, but they're just like, 'the long run, the long run.' So taking it a game at a time, a day at a time, and attacking it. Everything's fine. I don't look at (social) media like that, but yes, everything's fine. My body's great, just continuing to build."

Brown has yet to play both legs of a back to back, with the Nets to play Sunday in Dallas.

Mikel Brown Jr. looks to shoot over Zuby Ejiofor during the Nets' road loss to the Hawks. AP

He entered Saturday averaging 12 points and four assists. And the game is visibly slowing down for the rookie, mustering 14.5 points in his last four tilts.

"I'm very happy with Mikel. Him in pick-and-roll, how fast he is, how shifty, he can shoot the 3, get to the rim. Very happy with his performance," coach Jordi Fernández said.

"It's always about playing hard, but it's also about playing smarter as well. So, just really having the game slow down," Brown told The Post. "I watched a lot of film with my coaches, a whole lot of film for myself, just slowing down and letting the game come to me and just letting the game flow.

"And just staying aggressive, living with the consequences if I turn the ball over, miss an assignment on defense, just knowing I have to get it back the next play. Just always having that next-play mentality. That's the mindset that's been in my head these past couple games."

Brown has particularly excelled in the pick-and-roll, capped by an 18 point, four-assist outing in their win over Charlotte. He shot 6 of 11, 3 of 5 from deep and had a highlight no-look pass to Moritz Wagner off a baseline drive.

"Yeah, really the willingness to just be myself and create plays within that pick-and-roll situation, whether that's hitting the pocket, reading the tag man. Just allowing the game to come to me. It's nothing I haven't worked on for freaking years before this. So I adapt and put it on show," Brown said.

"Great player. Big-time offensive spark," said Terance Mann. "He's gonna be good when he gets older, stronger, understands the game, understands the pick-and-roll situations. I could see him being an All-Star easily. He has a great basketball mind. So, it's fun to play with him."

The Nets are strengthening Brown's body, especially his core, searching for that All-Star outcome.

#2838. Freiburg third after beating Union, Leverkusen beat Cologne in derby

Freiburg kept hot on the heels of Bayern Munich and Borussia Dortmund with a comfortable 2-0 home win over lowly Union Berlin to return to third in the Bundesliga table on Sunday, while Bayer Leverkusen sunk derby rivals Cologne.

Freiburg pressured Union captain Rani Khedira into an own goal nine minutes after half-time and Yuito Suzuki scored another as the hosts won despite their own skipper Vincenzo Grifo missing a penalty.

Last season's beaten Europa League finalists are third on 19 points, two behind Bayern and Dortmund with the Bavarians ahead on goal difference.

"We knew our chances would come and we managed to convert them well," Freiburg winger Derry Scherhant told DAZN.

After an even first-half, Freiburg were impressive after the break and forced a last-ditch intervention from Khedira, who knocked the ball into his own net.

Scherhant chipped for Suzuki to score with 67 minutes gone. The Black Forest side may have won by a higher margin were it not for Union goalkeeper Frederik Ronnow, who kept out Grifo's 84th-minute penalty and made a number of other saves.

Earlier on Sunday, goals from Aleix Garcia and Patrik Schick took Bayer Leverkusen to a tense 2-1 derby win at Cologne which lifted the visitors to fifth in the table.

The fiery encounter produced 11 yellow cards, including six for the home side.

One of several derbies in Germany's football-mad north-west, Cologne and Leverkusen are just 10 kilometres (six miles) apart.

The travelling support teased the hosts with chants referring to the 2023-24 season, when Leverkusen won their maiden Bundesliga title and Cologne were relegated.

Leverkusen broke the deadlock just seven minutes in when Garcia's free-kick flew over the wall and into the top-left corner, with a helpless Cologne goalie Marvin Schwaebe watching on.

Just before the break, the Spaniard found Jarell Quansah in space but the England centre-back headed just wide.

Well handled by the Leverkusen defence, the hosts grew increasingly frustrated, picking up five of their six yellow cards in the second-half.

Schick had a goal ruled out for handball with ten minutes to go but broke through just five minutes later, slotting in on the counter for an eighth goal in nine league appearances this season.

Germany winger Said El Mala scored one of his own in stoppage time but the goal came too late to launch a Cologne comeback and left the hosts stranded in 15th, two points above Union who sit in the relegation play-off spot.

In Sunday's late game, Hoffenheim won 2-1 at promoted Schalke, with Fisnik Asllani and Tim Lemperle getting on the scoresheet for the hosts.

#2835. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2834. Warriors keep streak alive, win seventh straight behind Steph Curry's 41 points

SAN FRANCISCO – It wasn't long ago that the Golden State Warriors were being written off, calling the 2026-27 season a throwaway after an 0-3 start.

They've since won seven consecutive games and have slowly become one of the hottest teams in the league. If you were sleeping on Golden State, then it's time to wake up. The boys are balling.

The Warriors kept their win streak intact, battling the Orlando Magic in a 126-119 overtime win on Saturday, Nov. 7 at Chase Center in San Francisco.

The Magic came into the with just two wins and were desperate for a spark. It was an easy situation for the Warriors to overlook. Golden State came out the gates with a 16 point lead but Orlando battled back and made it a game throughout.

"These games always scare me when people are out for the other team (with injury) because the league is filled with hungry, young, athletic players and the way everybody plays now, it's speed, crash the glass, and create extra possessions," Kerr said. "We only got out of there alive because of Steph (Curry) and then some great individual efforts by guys.  We got totally outplayed that whole second half. They were amazing. 33 more field-goal attempts. It's almost impossible to win a game, but I'm proud of our guys, because they gutted it out."

Golden State was led by their veteran superstar Stephen Curry, who scored 12 unanswered points himself in the final 2:21 of regulation en route to a 41-point performance. It was his 16th 40-point game since turning 35 years old. He has the most 40-point games in NBA history by a player age 35 or older.

Curry had a vintage performance in which he gets hot in a flash and becomes unstoppable. He went 13-of-19 from the field, 5-of-10 on 3-point shots and a perfect 10-of-10 at the free throw line. He was the engine for the Warriors, as he's been time and time again throughout his career. It doesn't get old to someone like Draymond Green who's played alongside Curry since 2012.

"It never gets old," Green told USA TODAY Sports. "He's so special, like, he always does something to where it can't get old.  You know, it's always something different. ... Down the stretch, you kind of get to a space where you just expect him to make those plays. But never try to take it for granted."

Green caught himself in awe of Curry on one particular shot when he was being guarded by Magic forward Jamal Cain as the shot clock was winding down with 6:46 left in the third quarter. Cain was all over Curry and still, Curry found space to hoist up a 3-point try and it found the bottom of the net.

"The guy's the greatest shooter ever played basketball. But I don't know what in your mind would just tell you, oh, this guy's pressuring me, just shoot," Green said. "Right? That's crazy. I can't begin to process those thoughts, you know, where you're like, gosh, right here. I'm gonna just shoot it. Most people, they do that and you look at them like, yo, what are you doing? It goes in. So I would say that was the one tonight that was really like, man, it's crazy. But, you know, I try I make it a point to try to never take it for granted."

Curry is going to get his but the battle was won through their depth. Every player who logged minutes for Golden State score. The Warriors bench outscored Orlando's second unit, 42-31.

After Curry, Warriors rookie Yaxel Lendeborg had the next high with 23 points on 9-of-15 shooting from the field. Brandin Podziemski added 15 points and 7 rebounds.

The Warriors look to continue their hot streak as they host the New Orleans Pelicans on Monday, Nov. 9 at Chase Center in San Francisco, California. Tip-off begins at 7 p.m. PT (10 p.m. ET).

#2832. Bears vs Buccaneers live updates, start time, TV channel, stream, where to watch NFL game

The Tampa Bay Buccaneers and Chicago Bears collide under the lights at Soldier Field on Sunday night.

Both teams enter Week 9 with plenty of momentum, but they have taken very different paths to get here.

Tampa Bay has dramatically turned its season around after an ugly 0-4 start. Baker Mayfield battled through a dislocated thumb that sidelined him for three games, but the Buccaneers have rattled off back-to-back wins since his return.

There's just one rather significant caveat: Mayfield has attempted a combined 14 passes in those victories. In fact, Tampa Bay became the first team since 1974 to win a game without completing a pass, leaning heavily on its ground game and defense. Still, the Bucs are in the thick of a wide-open NFC South, and a win tonight would send a strong message that they're the team to beat in the division.

Standing in their way is a Chicago team that looks every bit like a legitimate Super Bowl contender.

Quarterback Caleb Williams is healthy again after missing three games earlier this season with a hamstring injury, and Ben Johnson's squad is firing on all cylinders. The Bears are fresh off a statement Monday night victory over the defending Super Bowl champions, with their defense looking every bit as dangerous as their high-powered offense. Trade acquisition defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons made an immediate impact in his debut and could be the missing piece for Dennis Allen's unit.

Chicago looks like the top team in the NFC right now, but can the Bears avoid a letdown at Soldier Field? Or will Tampa Bay continue its remarkable resurgence and make another statement?

#2830. FIA delivers double penalty following Brazilian GP investigations

The FIA has confirmed its final decisions on two incidents involving Charles Leclerc and Oliver Bearman at the Brazilian Grand Prix, with both drivers coming under scrutiny after the race.

Leclerc was investigated for making contact with Liam Lawson at Turn 12, with the stewards concluding that the incident gave the Ferrari driver an advantage in their battle for position.

Although Lawson initially remained ahead, Leclerc managed to overtake him five corners later. The stewards determined that the contact allowed the Monegasque to close the gap and ultimately complete the move.

Lawson argued that the collision damaged the left-rear rim of his car, while Leclerc maintained that the contact was minor and carried no significant sporting consequences.

However, the stewards concluded that the collision disrupted Lawson's race and helped Leclerc get close enough to challenge for the position. They also established that the Racing Bulls driver was entitled to the corner, meaning Leclerc was responsible for avoiding contact.

The wet conditions and relatively minor nature of the incident were taken into consideration, resulting in a five-second penalty rather than a ten-second sanction. As a result, Leclerc drops from seventh to eighth in the final classification, with Lawson gaining one position.

Oliver Bearman was handed a separate five-second penalty following an unsafe release involving Leclerc in the pit lane.

The Haas driver was released from his pit box directly into the path of the Ferrari, which was already travelling along the pit lane. According to the stewards, Leclerc was forced to brake and take evasive action, moving towards the pit wall on the right-hand side to avoid a collision.

After reviewing the available footage, the officials concluded that Haas released Bearman in an unsafe manner, resulting in a five-second penalty. Unlike Leclerc, however, Bearman's penalty does not affect his final finishing position.

#2829. Broncos WR Jaylen Waddle, CB Riley Moss ruled out vs. Panthers with concussions

Denver Broncos wide receiver Jaylen Waddle and cornerback Riley Moss both left Sunday's 26-21 victory against the Carolina Panthers with concussions.

Waddle took the contact to his head in the first quarter while trying to catch a pass over the middle from Bo Nix in between two defenders. Safety Zakee Wheatley hit Waddle's facemask with the crown of his helmet as Waddle leapt for the ball.

Waddle entered the sideline medical tent after the contact and was evaluated on the field for a concussion. He then jogged to the Broncos' locker room.

Later in the first half, Moss took a knee to the head while making a tackle on a run play.

Moss remained down on the field in pain for several moments before being evaluated for a concussion.

The Broncos went into halftime with a 13-7 lead. They ruled both Waddle and Moss out for the game after halftime, leaving them without starters on both sides of the ball.

#2828. Kenny Atkinson takes jokes as lesson for ‘analytically’ comment in last season’s playoff loss to Knicks

CLEVELAND — Kenny Atkinson has been getting mocked for months. The jokes at the Cavaliers coach's expense — courtesy of Josh Hart, Karl-Anthony Towns and countless others — could continue for years to come.

After losing the first three games of the Eastern Conference finals, Atkinson infamously said that the Cavaliers had "analytically" won two of the first three games against the Knicks, but the Long Island native wishes the question that prompted his comment — regarding what hope the Cavaliers had of becoming the first NBA team to overcome a 3-0 series deficit — had as long a lifespan.

"When you take the context of the question, I'm not sure that was taken into account, that's a little bit of a lesson for me, you can just take a sound bite," Atkinson said.

"There was no coach more complimentary of the Knicks and honest about our performance and how they dominated us … 99 percent of my comments were how they dominated us. They were by far the better team."

Even if some of the numbers were on Cleveland's side, Atkinson acknowledges that so much of the difference in the lopsided series couldn't be measured.

Kenny Atkinson reacts during the Knicks' 109-97 win over the Cavaliers on Nov. 7, 2026 in Cleveland. Getty Images

"There's the mental toughness, the physical toughness, how aggressive you are, all these other intangibles that go into that," Atkinson said. "In no sense was it like, 'Oh man, we deserve to be winning.' There was zero thought of that."

The Knicks traveled without Hart, who missed his second consecutive game due to right ankle soreness despite practicing with the team the previous day in New York.

The Knicks have not set a timetable for Hart's return, but coach Mike Brown expressed no long-term concern, noting that the team's schedule — featuring one game in a five-day span — played a role in giving Hart additional time to recover.

"It's nothing major," Brown said. "He did go through some of practice [Friday]. I set him out of some contact, so we just want to take advantage where we are schedule-wise."

Hart, who missed eight games last season with a right ankle injury, also missed the team's preseason finale Oct. 15 due to the same issue. The 31-year-old started each of the team's first seven games of the season, most recently suiting up in Monday's win against the Nets. He is averaging 8.6 points — his fewest in eight years — while shooting a career-worst 38.6 percent from the field.

Landry Shamet, who scored nine points, made his second consecutive start in Hart's place.

Brown named Andre Drummond the Knicks' best defensive player in the 109-97 win.

The backup big man had eight rebounds, two steals and one block in 14 minutes

Jose Alvarado recorded a DNP-CD, while Tyler Kolek backed up Jalen Brunson, finishing with five assists, four rebounds and one steal in 14 minutes

#2827. Virginia vs Arkansas men's basketball game was one for the birds – literally

The first full week of the 2026-27 men's college basketball season was capped off by a highly anticipated matchup between No. 10 Virginia and No. 13 Arkansas in Washington, D.C on Sunday, Nov. 8.

It was the kind of game that everyone wanted to get in on the action for – and not just the nearly 11,000 fans who were inside Capital One Arena.

With 2:09 remaining in the second half, and with the Cavaliers nursing a comfortable 83-68 lead, the game was stopped briefly due to a pair of pigeons that had made their way onto the court.

More: 3 takeaways from Arkansas basketball's blowout loss to Virginia

Initial attempts to shoo the birds away were unsuccessful. Razorbacks junior guard Billy Richmond III, apparently upset that something on the court could fly higher than him and his 41-inch vertical leap, lowered his left hand to hover above the court and try to usher the pigeons away. But he only moved them to a different portion of the court, where they continued to loiter.

Eventually, with the birds near the baseline, a group of three Arkansas players and a charging John Calipari were able to get them to fly off.

The pigeons flew toward the rafters and didn't appear to leave the arena, but the game was able to finish without any added interruptions beyond the referee's whistle.

Virginia finished off a dominant performance to earn a 91-72 victory. All five Cavaliers starters finished in double figures, led by Thijs De Ridder and Sam Lewis, who had 16 points apiece.

#2823. Jaxon Smith-Njigba can do something no one has done since 1960

Maybe this year they’ll send Jaxon Smith-Njigba a trophy without a typo.

The Seahawks receiver, through eight games, remains on pace to become the first player to generate more than 2,000 receiving yards in a single season.

His 985 yards project to 2,093 yards for the full season.

The current record is held by Hall of Famer Calvin Johnson, who gained 1,964 yards in 2012 with the Lions.

And for those who would argue that an asterisk should apply based on the extension of the season to 17 games, Smith-Njigba’s current production puts him on pace for 1,970 yards in 16 games.

There’s something more immediate that Smith-Njigba can achieve. With 115 receiving yards and a touchdown against the Cardinals on Sunday, Smith-Njigba will become only the fourth player in NFL history with 1,100 receiving yards and 10 touchdowns through the first nine games of a season.

The others were Hall of Famer Don Hutson in 1942, Hall of Famer Elroy “Crazy Legs” Hirsch in 1951, and Hall of Famer Raymond Berry in 1960.

It’s amazing that it hasn’t happened in 66 years. With the rules that loosened the passing game in the 1970s, and given the many great receivers who have played over the past six decades (Jerry Rice, Randy Moss, Terrell Owens, just to name three), no one has done what Smith-Njigba could do today.

#2821. Eagles take 8-6 lead over Giants into halftime

The first half of Sunday’s game between the Giants and Eagles was a pretty dull affair, but it did feature a very memorable punt.

Eagles punter Braden Mann had to boot the ball away from his own end zone with seconds to play in the second quarter and he crushed a kick that sailed over Giants punt returner Charlie Jones’s head. The ball wound up going out of bounds on the Giants’ 6-yard-line and went into the books as an 86-yard punt.

That’s the longest in the NFL since the 2001 season and it assured the Giants would take a knee rather than try to score some late points before halftime. That left the Eagles up 8-6 in their bid for a 14th straight home win over their divisional rivals.

Former Giant Saquon Barkley scored the only touchdown of the day when he sprinted into the end zone from three yards out in the first quarter. The Eagles made it 8-6 on a Joe Milton sneak after a penalty allowed them to try for two points from the 1-yard-line.

Barkley has 10 carries for 22 yards and Milton has run for 24 yards while going 8-of-14 for 87 yards. Milton also lost a fumble on an Arvell Reese sack and the Giants first-rounder added a second sack later in the half.

Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy moved the team to two field goals on their first two possessions, but the Giants punted four times while picking up one first down over the rest of the half. McCarthy has been sacked five times with Zack Baun getting him twice and the Giants have just 16 rushing yards on 10 attempts.

That offensive futility on both sides helped make Mann’s punt the biggest highlight of the first 30 minutes in Philly.

#2820. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2815. Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30

Observations after Sixers earn strong road win over Pistons, Brown scores 30 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers remain the Eastern Conference's sole leader.

They earned a 112-106 road win over the Pistons on Wednesday night to move to 8-1 on the season and stay at the top of the standings. Detroit fell to 6-2.

The Sixers last started 8-1 in the 2023-24 season, which was Nick Nurse's first as the team's head coach.

Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 30 points and pulled down seven rebounds.

Joel Embiid had 26 points and a season-best 15 rebounds. LeBron James posted 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. Tyrese Maxey added 19 points and seven assists.

The Pistons' leading scorers were Duncan Robinson with 27 points and Cade Cunningham with 26.

The Sixers were down Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pistons' injured players included Jalen Duren (right hamstring strain) and Isaiah Joe (right hip contusion).

Nurse told reporters in Detroit he thinks "it'll be another 10 to 12 days before they kind of clear (Barlow) for getting on the court to see where he is," according to Sixers Wire's Ky Carlin.

The Sixers will visit the Cavaliers on Friday night. Here are observations on their victory over Detroit:

Embiid assisted the Sixers' first basket with a sharp pass out of a double team. He saw Brown's cut along the baseline and dished to him for a layup.

Detroit ran several double drag screen actions early, forcing Embiid to defend on the perimeter. The Pistons started 7 for 11 from the floor and took a 17-11 edge on a Robinson three-pointer.

The Sixers then made a 10-0 run and Embiid was right in the middle of it. He converted a put-back layup after a pretty high-low feed from James. Embiid hit two foul shots to give the Sixers the lead. The team raised its defensive level and Detroit's offense grew frigid.

Embiid was very good on the glass in the first half, grabbing eight rebounds. The Sixers had an excellent rebounding game as a team, limiting the Pistons to four offensive boards over 48 minutes.

Embiid didn't break out of his early-season three-point shooting slump, going 0 for 3. He's 3 for 26 (11.5 percent) on the season and hasn't made a three since the Sixers' second game.

Late in the second quarter, Embiid got into a dead-ball dust-up with Tolu Smith. After an officials' review, Embiid was assessed a technical foul for "initiating" the altercation.

Playing on his 26th birthday, Maxey missed his first four field goals and was scoreless until he hit a floater with 31.5 seconds left in the first quarter.

Brown picked up the scoring slack, tallying 10 points in the first on 4-for-6 shooting and hitting a deep, buzzer-beating three to put the Sixers up 29-21. He produced at all three levels, chose smart spots to attack and looked comfortable in the rhythm of the Sixers' offense.

Meanwhile, James did his usuall stellar work as a passer. He assisted two Adem Bona slams in the second quarter and constantly spotted open teammates. Nurse rewarded Bona's strong opening stint, playing him for a lengthy stretch.

Brown swished a corner triple to extend the Sixers' lead to 48-31. With a 3-for-5 night, Brown is at 45.7 percent from long distance so far this season. It wouldn't be surprising at all if Brown's three-point shooting improved from last season's 34.7 percent clip. With such an abundance of star power, the Sixers' hope will be that everyone gets regular open looks beyond the arc throughout the year.

As for Maxey, he sat with 3:26 left in the second quarter after being whistled for his third foul. Even with a mere two points from Maxey, the Sixers entered halftime holding a 14-point advantage.

The Pistons played better basketball to begin the third quarter.

Following a John Collins alley-oop jam, Nurse called timeout with his team's lead down to single digits. The Pistons' offense sustained some success, but the Sixers prevented a sustained run.

Embiid played an especially physical, aggressive third quarter. He tipped in his own miss and Maxey then sunk a three to make the Sixers' lead 84-68. They were up as many as 17 points in the third.

Embiid sat out the fourth quarter Monday night for the Sixers' win over the Bulls and said he was on a minutes restriction (25) because of his right foot plantar fasciitis. He played the fourth period against the Pistons and logged 33 minutes. Bona went back to the locker room early in the fourth. Nurse told reporters postgame that Bona had "neck soreness."

Detroit did not let the Sixers coast to victory.

Robinson drained a three. Cunningham made a pair of free throws and drove in for a layup that cut the Pistons' deficit to 106-102.

The Sixers halted the Pistons' run before anything disastrous was on the table. Brown blocked a Cunningham three-point try, Embiid knocked down two foul shots, and the East's No. 1 early-season team improved to 4-0 on the road.

#2808. Chiefs-Jets: 4 instant takeaways from the bounce-back victory

In Week 9, the Kansas City Chiefs defeated the New York Jets 35-12 to move to 6-2. Here are 4 instant takeaways from the game:

After the Chiefs' first two losses of the season, both games featured far less of running back Kenneth Walker than the initial five-game start, the offense got back on track by featuring its top playmaker. The lead back finished with 143 yards from scrimmage and three touchdowns over 22 touches.

Most of his production came through the air: for his first score, he leaked into the opposite flat in the red zone and outflanked his coverage responsibility to finish off a seven-yard touchdown pass. On the second, he caught a screen pass and, with great blocks from right guard Trey Smith and left guard Kingsley Suamataia, ran down the right sideline into the end zone from 25 yards away.

Still, his carries showcased his individual talent the most. As he chipped away at 102 rushing yards, he was constantly staying up through contact or making defenders grasp at air with his elusiveness. The threat of his gashes opened things up against the Denver Broncos last week, and that continued alongside his own impactful gains against New York.

It certainly made a difference to have Suamataia back on the field, but the game plan to lean on Walker was smart and effective. He is the engine of an in-rhythm offense in Kansas City.

Whatever criticism the Chiefs' pass rush faced heading into this game, it was silenced.

Defensive tackle Chris Jones needs to be more impactful? How about two big-time sacks all by himself? He beat a guard quickly on one play, then flew around the right edge before plowing quarterback Geno Smith into the grass in the fourth quarter.

Defensive end George Karlaftis needs to do more than bat balls down? How about finishing with two sacks, another tackle for loss, and a separate quarterback hit? Oh yeah, and he also batted a pass down. He is now the team leader in both passes defended (9) and sacks (4).

Other players need to win around Jones and Karlaftis? Defensive end Ashton Gillotte was relentless on his way to a sack that cost the offense 14 yards. Kansas City had five sacks, and none of them came from a blitz.

Right tackle Jaylon Moore (ankle) left the game in the second half and didn't return; Joshua Ezeudu replaced him. Before he went out, Moore was having a very hard time with Jets' rookie edge rusher David Bailey — the No. 2 pick in this year's draft. The phenom finished with two sacks, two forced fumbles, and an additional quarterback hit.

The biggest hit he allowed left Mahomes with a hurt throwing elbow late in the first half. The two-time MVP walked to the sideline with his right arm hanging loose, but never missed a snap and didn't let the injury noticeably hamper him more than the constant pressure itself did.

The Jets registered eight quarterback hits and two sacks over 34 dropbacks by Mahomes, despite the offense having a rhythm for much of the game. It speaks to Kansas City's lack of answers for an elite edge rusher like Bailey when those matchups come up in the future.

After the 51-yard miss in perfect football conditions at his home stadium — which followed a 56-yard miss negated by a penalty — the era of kicker Harrison Butker feels all but over. Get ready for Justin Tucker to kick for the team against the Atlanta Falcons in Week 10.
