#2806. Diving deeper into Cavs 109-97 loss to Knicks: The more things change, the more they stay the same

CLEVELAND — The Cleveland Cavaliers couldn't keep up with a New York Knicks team that just has their number. They dropped Saturday's game 109-97.

Brunson is a certified Cavs killer. He picked up right where he left off last postseason by torching whatever defender Cleveland put in front of him. He got inside the paint and to his midrange floater with ease. And when he didn't, the outside shot was working as well.

There really isn't much that you can do when Brunson has it going as he did here. The Cavs sent some doubles at him and tried to shade their defense toward him. When they got too far, he made the easy pass to the open teammate, never forcing anything.

The Cavs' strategy was fine. The defense also wasn't necessarily horrible. Cleveland's guards competed, but that didn't matter. Brunson is just too good a shot and decision-maker.

37 points, 4 assists, 4 rebounds, 2 steals, 1 block

Mitchell is putting together an impressive season from a process standpoint. He's doing a better job of looking for his teammates, while also finding avenues to score.

"I think the biggest thing is creating an opportunity," Mitchell said. "I haven't shot as many shots as I have in my career, so trying to balance that and figure out trying to make the right basketball play. Sometimes that is shooting. Sometimes that is passing. Sometimes that is running the blitz, and it's going to help to make me better."

Mitchell tried to will the Cavs back into this game, scoring 21 in the final quarter alone. But as impressive as he was, a one-man army wasn't enough to take down a well-oiled machine like the Knicks. Especially when the rest of his co-stars struggled.

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the Spida shirt HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cavs gear HERE.

29 points, 13 rebounds, 3 assists, 2 steals

Towns is such a difficult player to defend. His outside shot requires him to be guarded out on the perimeter, which opens up the lane for his teammates. If the defensive center leaves, Towns can make them pay with the outside shot. And, if his defender closes out too hard, Towns is quick enough off the dribble to make them pay inside.

This was on display on Saturday. He drew two quick fouls on Mobley in the first quarter by attacking the rim. This changed the complexion of the game. Without Jarrett Allen, the Cavs were forced to turn to third-string center Thomas Bryant for a longer stretch than they would've ideally liked.

Towns's presence can completely change an offense as dynamic as New York's. Cleveland found that out again the hard way.

7 points on 2-of-9 shooting, 5 assists, 2 rebounds, 2 blocks

The Cavs offense can look lifeless when Harden isn't able to get downhill. On Saturday, he was a step slow and couldn't create any separation off the dribble. That limited his effectiveness.

On the other end, the Knicks continually hunted him in mismatches. To his credit, Harden competed. But it wasn't enough to keep this from being a winning strategy.

This has been less than an ideal start to the season for Harden.

12 points, 9 rebounds, 5 assists, 3 turnovers

Mobley's ability to win the matchup over Joel Embiid was a deciding factor in Cleveland's runaway win over the Philadelphia 76ers. Likewise, his inability to do so against New York contributed to the loss.

The foul trouble made it difficult for Mobley to establish any kind of offense inside. This left him as mostly a jump shooter at the start of the game. And since the three-ball wasn't falling (1-for-5 on the evening), there wasn't much he was capable of offensively to help jumpstart an offense that needed it.

Watson was useful in this game. He provided some much-needed offense and was helpful as a defender. That's what you want to see from Watson in this matchup.

"Not really. In Jauary we went 2-9. I'm not really into measuring stick games). … Let's say we win by 15. Well, we might get hammered next week by whoever's in last place by 20. Does that mean we suck? No. You just take it one game at a time and try to get a little bit better."

– Knicks head coach Mike Brown before the game when asked if this was a measuring stick game

It's tempting to use this difficult stretch of games as a test for where the Cavs are. And if you were going to use it as one, they wouldn't necessarily pass.

The more concerning thing is the lack of discernible identity. When they're playing well, the Cavs can beat anyone, as we saw against Philadelphia. But what does this group hang their hat on? What does better look like for them? And are they taking steps to get there? Those are the questions that matter, and we don't have clear answers for any of them.

The Knicks outscored the Cavs 26-8 in second-chance points.

New York retrieved 41.8% of their missed shots (94th percentile). And then they made the most of those opportunities by scoring a quarter's worth of points on that alone.

"We have to rebound better collectively," head coach Kenny Atkinson said. "There's no one pointing the finger. The guys got to do it collectively. We just got to be better."

I don't necessarily think the Knicks present stylistic problems that the Cavs can't solve. An offense that hunts out mismatches with their smaller, but electric, scoring guard isn't something the Cavs should, on paper, be incapable of countering. Yet, the Cavs are consistently ending up on the wrong side of this matchup. The Knicks have their number, and there's no way around that.

Atkinson and Mitchell were asked what makes this matchup so difficult afterward. Both pointed to the offensive rebounding as an obvious issue, and one that we all can see. They also both mentioned that the Knicks just understand how to play with each other and use that to seize the openings teams give them.

"They really have solutions," Atkinson said. "I think their continuity…they just have great chemistry. They're on a string on both ends."

"They've been through stuff together as a group," Mitchell said. "They do a good job os seizing mistakes."

Brunson and Towns were tremendous once again tonight. Their shotmaking carried them in this matchup. But they also did a great job of accepting what the Cavs threw at them, and then having a plan of action for that. If you doubled Brunson, he moved the ball to the open teammate and made the Cavs' defense scramble out.

Conversely, when they threw doubles at Mitchell, the Cavs weren't quick to respond. Watson admitted afterward that he doesn't know to help Mitchell in that situation and that there are "some questions" about their strategy that he would like to ask about. That isn't the sign of a team that's on a string and understands what they need to do to be the best version of themselves.

Little things like that are why the Knicks have been better. They understand what they need to do, and the pieces fit together perfectly to make that happen. The Cavs don't have that right now. And until they do, they won't be able to get through the roadblock the Knicks have become throughout this era of Cavs basketball.

#2802. Phoenix bent but never broke against the Rockets

Saturday night's victory over the Houston Rockets was easily the Phoenix Suns' best win of the season. Granted, they've only won three games through their first eight, but this one felt different. Phoenix continued its recent trend of putting together quality first quarters, something that has become increasingly important given how frequently this team has found itself playing from behind. When the Suns fall into an early deficit, players tend to press, forcing the issue offensively and abandoning the systems and schemes designed to put them in positions to succeed.

But Saturday night presented a different kind of adversity. After building a lead that reached as many as 19 points, the Suns completely unraveled in the second quarter. They managed a miserable 12 points, shot 3-of-11 from beyond the arc, and apparently decided to let amber be the color of their energy as Houston closed the half on a 22-3 run to erase the deficit entirely (that's a 311 joke). What had been a commanding Phoenix advantage was suddenly a tie game heading into the locker room.

And that's where things got interesting. Throughout the second half, we witnessed a collection of quality performances from different players on both ends of the floor. There were timely offensive contributions, defensive adjustments, and moments when individual effort helped stabilize a team that had every reason to start questioning itself. By the time the final buzzer sounded, the Suns had earned a 102-99 victory over one of the NBA's most potent offenses.

That's right. For the first time this season, Phoenix held an opponent under 100 points. And that opponent happened to be the Houston Rockets.

That accomplishment alone makes this victory impressive, but what stands out even more is how the Suns responded after their complete second-quarter meltdown. When you surrender a 19-point advantage in such a short period of time, it's easy to fold. It's easy to begin doubting yourself, questioning whether you have the personnel, discipline, or defensive capability to stop the opponent standing across from you. We've seen that uncertainty creep into this team's play far too often through the opening weeks of the season.

Saturday night, however, was the first time we truly witnessed the mental toughness and fortitude that defined this team a season ago. The Suns refused to let one disastrous quarter dictate the outcome of the game. They regrouped, stood tall, and collectively found a way to shut down a Houston offense that had been operating like a well-oiled machine. In doing so, they earned, in every sense of the word, their third victory of the season.

Booker is up to three BSB's after his 30-point aggressive performance against the Magic.

#2801. Fantasy Football Injury Ramifications: J.J. McCarthy Injury Opens Door For Jameis Winston Return

J.J. McCarthy had the chance to lead the New York Giants' offense against the Philadelphia Eagles, but a hip injury saw McCarthy briefly leave the game.

The Giants may have to turn to Jameis Winston to lead the charge in the coming weeks, which boosts Winston's value just several games after being benched for McCarthy.

Just weeks after being traded from the Minnesota Vikings, J.J. McCarthy was thrust into the starting quarterback role for the Giants.

McCarthy was clearly in pain against the Eagles after taking a hard hit, and he exited the game before eventually returning.

His injury could be a future concern for the Giants, so what would a return by Jameis Winston mean for the Giants' offense?

Winston had two impressive fantasy performances for managers who picked up the second-string quarterback, coming in Weeks 4 and 5.

Aside from those performances, Winston had been underwhelming for the Giants before his eventual benching for McCarthy.

Before he was benched, Winston was able to find good connections on the field, especially with Malik Nabers and Isaiah Likely.

If McCarthy were to miss time with his hip injury, Winston would need to bring back his A-game and rely on his best pass-catchers downfield.

Nabers has been the go-to guy for the Giants' offense, with both McCarthy and Winston at quarterback.

Nabers is averaging 17.2 fantasy points per game this season, with double-digit fantasy perfomances in all but two games through eight weeks of the season.

With Winston at quarterback, Nabers has had 42 receptions for 536 yards, along with two touchdowns in six games.

With McCarthy at quarterback, Nabers has had 30 receptions for 397 yards and one touchdown in three games.

Likely is having his best fantasy season this year, averaging 16.1 fantasy points over seven games.

Likely has been a TE1 this season, with four double-digit fantasy performances this season. He remains a TE1 no matter who the quarterback for the Giants is.

Likely has had 24 catches for 258 yards and four touchdowns in four career games with McCarthy at quarterback.

With Winston at quarterback, Likely has had 38 receptions for 370 yards and four touchdowns in seven games.

McCarthy has a higher fantasy ceiling for the Giants, although Winston's experience helps the veteran quarterback should he be needed.

Both Nabers and Likely remain must-start fantasy players for managers, no matter who is at quarterback for the Giants.

#2798. Open Thread: Jeremy Sochan’s first press conference after being drafted first overall in the G-League

What a ride Jeremy Sochan has had, and the new season is just getting underway. After nearly four seasons with the San Antonio Spurs, he was waived and signed with the Knicks, picking up a championship ring when New York beat the Spurs in the 2026 Finals.

After New York, it appeared Sochan was spending the 2026-2027 season in Portland, but was waived after four preseason games. The Polish-American power forward declared for the G-League draft and one week later was chosen first overall by the Texas Legends.

Here he is earlier this week in his first press conference as a member of the Legends.

"I think the way I grew up, my family, my parents; it's always been like that. I could look at it negatively (the Portland situation). But when I look back at that situation, I think the two months I was there, I grew a lot. I was healthy throughout the whole training camp, preseason and open gym. So I think that was a blessing. When we got into preseason games, the minutes I got I think I used them to the best of my ability. I had good games. So what would be the point of being negative and angry for getting waived? Of course, you can look at it as that's the NBA and I have a contract. But at the same time I have good momentum. So, why would I hold weight on something that I can't control? And instead, be in this moment and do what I can do and keep that momentum going."

According to Marc Spears, Sochan originally found out he was being waived by the Spurs through another player:

"I thought I was going to be there for the rest of the season at least. And a few days after the trade deadline, it's funny. I don't want to be messy or anything, but I found out from one of my teammates that they wanted to waive me, and it definitely hit me in a way. But again, I kind of had the mentality of, 'It is what it is.' I heard it was pretty possible from someone else, and then officially found out through my agent. So, that was definitely new to me."

Texas Legends season kicks off tonight in Mexico City against the Capitanes.

#2796. Winners and losers from Georgia football's 42-7 win at Ole Miss

The Georgia Bulldogs faced the Mississippi Rebels on the road in another highly anticipated SEC contest on Saturday. The Rebels had a 16-game home win streak, but the Bulldogs halted it, winning 42-7 to raise their record to 8-1 this season, while Mississippi is 6-3. Here are the winners and losers from the game.

Georgia's offense was electric during the game, scoring 42 total points. Quarterback Gunner Stockton went 9 for 15 for 186 yards and four touchdown passes. Wide receiver Talyn Taylor had a great performance, catching four passes for 140 yards and three touchdowns. Running back Nate Frazier looked great, leading the team in rushing with 107 yards on 16 carries.

Mississippi wide receiver Horatio Fields was one of the only bright spots for them on offense, catching eight passes for 96 yards. Fields led the team in receiving and was a reliable target for Trinidad Chambliss.

Georgia's defense looked fantastic against Ole Miss. The Bulldogs dominated the Ole Miss offensive front and shut down the Rebels until garbage time in the fourth quarter. Georgia sacked Ole Miss five times and forced a pair of Ole Miss turnovers.

Georgia kicker Peyton Woodring missed his lone field goal in the matchup. He missed a 49-yard field goal before halftime. This is the fourth consecutive game where he's missed. But he did make all of his point after attempts. Woodring has made nine of 13 kicks this season.

Mississippi's offense did not have a good game, scoring only seven points. Trinidad Chambliss was sacked five times. They weren't able to get much going. Chambliss was forced out of the pocket multiple times and threw an interception to Georgia safety K.J. Bolden. The Rebels' run game was nonexistent, rushing for 66 yards. The Ole Miss offensive line has a very poor day and could not defend Georgia's blitz.

Georgia will take on Missouri on Saturday, November 14th.

#2795. Five-star Leipzig recover from Augsburg's fast start to extend perfect home record

Despite conceding goals in the first and last minutes of the game, RB Leipzig managed to maintain their perfect home record with a 5-2 win over FC Augsburg.

#2793. Against Oklahoma City, the Wizards were the bug

Oklahoma City Thunder broadcaster Michael Cage summed up this game perfectly: "In this game, sometimes you're the bug, and sometimes you're the windshield." In this one, the Wizards were in the role of the bug.

Washington was no match for the Thunder. Oklahoma City took control early and the Wizards had no answer for their athleticism, execution, and swarming defensive presence.

The Wizards converted just 29.6% of their field goal attempts, including 7-43 from three-point range. That's 16.3%. Which is bad. Very bad.

Positives for the Wizards were scarce. Among them — Alex Sarr grabbed 14 rebounds in 28 minutes. Bub Carrington shot 3-3 from deep. Bilal Coulibaly defended Shai Gilgeous-Alexander pretty well in the first quarter — SGA had two points on o-3 shooting when Coulibaly left the game. Despite missing Jalen Williams and Chet Holmgren (in some order, their second and third best players), OKC is a special case. It's a championship-level team with something to prove. The Wizards need to learn to compete at that level, and the lesson of their last three games is that they're still not close.

That's not the end of world 10 games into the season. But they need to internalize that lesson and do the work to become competitive against high-level opponents.

Kudos to the OKC stadium operations team — when the Wizards starters got introduced, they played Harry Potter theme music. Good job.

The Wizards entered the game with the NBA's second best "in the paint" field goal percentage (62.2%). The Thunder had the NBA's second best defensive paint FG% (holding opponents to just 50.2%). Safe to say OKC won that battle.

In the first quarter, the Wizards ran a pick-and-roll option out of a horns set for AJ Dybantsa. He had a choice of going left or right. It didn't go great — he ended up battling his way into a mid-ranger, which he missed. The set was good, though.

Tre Mann got real minutes for the first time this season. He looked pretty good — quick, aggressive, athletic. I'd like to see more.

Jotted during the second quarter (could have been jotted anytime) — OKC defenders switch HARD.

Another second quarter note — OKC was doubling Trae Young to get the ball out of his hands. The other Wizards weren't able to take make plays, in part because OKC defenders hustled back to their matchups and rotated so perfectly.

I thought maybe the Wizards had a chance of making things respectable — a thought that disappeared when I saw their possession of the second half. It was a pick-and-roll for Kyshawn George, who got caught in no-man's land. The only teammate who cut was Coulibaly, who didn't get free. That should have triggered a cascade of movement, but the other guys stood around until George was forced to take a difficult shot, which he missed.

The Wizards tried a small-ball lineup in the second half. It got shredded by Aday Mara and the Thunder.

#2791. Mike Vick, Norfolk State lose after his order was ignored

Like a growing number of former NFL players, Mike Vick has become a college football coach. Unlike many college football coaches, a direct order from Vick wasn’t followed on Saturday.

With 1:34 to play during Saturday’s game between Vick’s Norfolk State and North Carolina Central, Norfolk State led, 28-23. Norfolk State had the ball, facing second and five from the 50. North Carolina Central had no timeouts remaining.

While a pair of knees wouldn’t have fully exhausted the remaining time, Vick told the offense to take a knee in victory formation. Two straight kneel-downs would have resulted in a punt with less than 15 seconds to play.

Instead, Norfolk State lined up in shotgun formation, the quarterback ran to the left, a defender hit him, and the quarterback fumbled.

North Carolina Central recovered at the Norfolk State 37. Five plays later, North Carolina scored a touchdown, on a 26-yard touchdown pass, making the score 29-28. (The two-point conversion attempt failed.)

Twenty-seven seconds remained. Norfolk State drove in position to attempt a game-winning field goal. The 37-yard try missed wide left.

“I don’t know what happened at the end of the game,” Vick told reporters after a loss that dropped Norfolk State to 4-6 on the season. “I put my headset down. I called victory formation. All we had to do was take a knee, kneel the clock out. That’s just situational football. I don’t know what happened in regards to that. I looked up and we was running the ball, and then I seen the fumble. But my final instructions was victory formation. That’s when you kneel it out. And they had, I think, zero timeouts left and the game was over practically.”

“Well, I put my headset down, and I took my headset off because all we had to do was just run it three times, or two times, or kneel it out. That’s called victory. I don’t know what happened. A run play was called. I looked up, I seen us running the ball. I don’t know what happened. I really don’t. I don’t know what the play call was. I don’t know why we even ran that play.

“People will look at that, and that’ll go on as a teaching tape for a lot of teams across the country for years to come. I hate it had to be us. But I called victory formation. Just letting everybody know that. I called victory.”

While the strategy wouldn’t have completely exhausted the clock, it was the right move. And, even if it wasn’t, the coach gave the order. The order wasn’t followed.

So what happened? Vick said he had taken off his headset and then later realized that a run play had been called. The fact that the offense went straight into shotgun formation suggests that it wasn’t the players going rogue. Apparently, offensive coordinator Brian Sheppard decided to ignore Vick’s directive.

Indeed, a somewhat chaotic postgame video (posted by Vick on Twitter) includes Vick telling his players “it was supposed to have been victory.”

The video of the moments before the play in question shows the offense gathered at the numbers near the Norfolk State bench, speaking to a group of coaches who were still wearing headsets. The coach without a headset (who appears to be Vick) walked to the sideline before turning around and returning to the meeting.

It’s clear that the coaches and players were having a discussion. From Vick’s perspective, there was nothing to discuss.

And while Vick claims his instruction was ignored, he seemed to be a little too nonchalant at a time when he should have realized that something more detailed than telling the quarterback to line up under center, receive the snap, and take a knee was being planned.

If Vick believes Sheppard or other coaches overruled the head coach, Vick should be issuing pink slips immediately. It’s possible, though, that Vick himself bears the bulk of the blame for not noticing that something other than the implementation of victory formation was coming.

Unfortunately the Jets did not fully take advantage of the golden opportunity. They failed to get a first down and had to settle for another field goal. The first quarter ended with the Chiefs knocking on the door, looking to go ahead with a first and goal at the Jets 7 yard line. On the first play of the second quarter Mahomes found Kenneth Walker for a touchdown and, despite all the good things that happened for the Jets early, the Chiefs had the lead at 7 – 6.

The Jets fought back. They drove the ball near the Chiefs 20 yard before three consecutive plays went backwards. The Jets settled for a 49 yard field goal to regain the lead. Midway through the second quarter the Jets had scored on all three drives to this point, yet had only nine points and a two point lead to show for it. If they didn't start converting scoring opportunities into touchdowns it was likely going to be another long day for the Jets.

The Jets went into the locker room trailing the Chiefs 14 – 12. They had left a lot of points on the field. They had dominated time of possession, scored four times, outgained the Chiefs, out-sacked the Chiefs, led the turnover battle, and still trailed. It would be a surprise if that didn't cost them the game in the end.

The Jets opened the third quarter on defense. It did not go well. The kickoff went out of bounds, giving the Chiefs great field position at the 40 yard line. From there the Chiefs went on a drive ending in a short touchdown pass from Mahomes to Travis Kelce. The extra point gave the Chiefs a 21 – 12 lead. The Jets offense responded with a three and out. Midway through the third quarter the Jets were in serious trouble.

Towards the end of the third quarter the Chiefs put together a drive ending in a Kenneth Walker touchdown run. Walker was single handedly dismantling the Jets. The extra point made the score 28 – 12 in favor of the Chiefs as the third quarter ended. The game was all but over.

On the Jets' next drive they moved the ball quickly downfield before Geno Smith was sacked on fourth down, ending the drive. The Jets trailed by 16 points with a little over 11 minutes left. Turn out the lights, the party was over. All those points left on the field in the first half had come back to bite the Jets in the second half.

Late in the fourth quarter the Chiefs tacked on another touchdown. They had now outscored the Jets 21 – 0 in the second half as they cruised to an easy victory.

The loss brought the Jets' record to 2-7. A 16th straight season without the playoffs is all but certain. Another November and December talking about next year and the draft looms. What fun awaits as the holiday season approaches!

The Jets limp home to take on the first place Buffalo Bills next week. That should be a blast.

Thankfully, with Nate Ament (19), Kasparas Jakučionis (20), Brayden Burries (21), Kel'el Ware (22), Ousmane Dieng (23), and Ryan Rollins (24), these Bucks have plenty of time. And at least a few of 'em are already ahead of the curve.

There you have it, BH fam. While these Bucks may be too nice, that's not a bad position to start from. After all, as Laurell K. Hamilton wrote in her 2005 novel A Stroke of Midnight:

My father had taught me to be nice first, because you can always be mean later, but once you've been mean to someone, they won't believe the nice anymore. So be nice, be nice, until it's time to stop being nice, then destroy them.

That's right, the Bucks might be in the trenches, but a fire brews.

#2779. From Madrid to Mainz: Lennart Karl strikes again as Bayern extend unbeaten run

Bayern Munich maintained their unbeaten start to the Bundesliga season with a 2-1 victory away to Mainz 05. Goals from Joshua Kimmich and Lennart Karl put the visitors in control, although a late strike from substitute Philipp Tietz ensured a nervy finish.

The Bavarians ultimately secured all three points to head into the international break on a positive note.

Here is our official match report for the game:

Mainz had a little extra motivation heading into Saturday's clash. Urs Fischer, who made his debut as Mainz coach in last season's 2-2 draw against Bayern, was hoping to cause another upset. After all, the hosts had won four of their previous six home meetings with the record champions - a remarkable record against the Bundesliga giants.

Fischer also had a special message for Nadiem Amiri. Left out of Germany coach Jürgen Klopp's squad for the upcoming Nations League fixtures, the Mainz boss encouraged his star midfielder to use the disappointment as motivation and "show everyone what he's capable of".

Unfortunately for the hosts, Bayern had other plans.

The visitors dominated possession and looked the more dangerous side from the opening minutes. Mainz tried to disrupt their rhythm and create opportunities through set pieces, but Bayern eventually found a way through in the 14th minute.

A misplaced pass in Mainz's build-up handed Harry Kane the opportunity to launch a counterattack. As Sano stepped out to challenge the Englishman, Joshua Kimmich was left completely unmarked in the centre. Ballon d'Or winner Kane picked him out perfectly and the Bayern star made no mistake, smashing the ball underneath the crossbar to put the visitors 1-0 ahead.

Bayern continued to create chances but, much like in recent weeks, struggled to convert several promising opportunities.

Mainz thought they had found their equaliser shortly before the break. Amiri delivered a dangerous free-kick into the box, Jonathan Tah got the slightest touch, and Manuel Neuer missed his attempt to reach the ball. Lee was waiting at the back post and headed it over the line.

However, the celebrations were short-lived. An offside position in the build-up ruled out the goal.

Neuer appeared to pick up an injury during the set-piece incident and was unable to continue. Jonas Urbig replaced the veteran goalkeeper before half-time.

The second half initially offered little in terms of excitement, but Bayern remained firmly in control.

Then came the 57th minute. Konrad Laimer spotted Harry Kane making a run in behind and delivered a superb long ball towards the striker. Kane got his shot away, but after the rebound fell back into his path, he cleverly squared it for Lennart Karl.

The teenager made no mistake, firing home to double Bayern's advantage.

After finding the net against Atlético Madrid in the Champions League, Karl was at it again – this time in Mainz. Not a bad week for the young Bayern sensation!

The goal put Bayern in a comfortable position for the remainder of the match. The visitors appeared to tire somewhat, particularly given their demanding schedule and recent injury concerns.

Bayern appeared to have the game under control in the closing stages, but Mainz made a late attempt to turn things around. Substitute Phillip Tietz pulled one back for the hosts in the 92nd minute, setting up a tense finish. However, the late goal proved insufficient to prevent Bayern from taking all three points.

The victory provides a welcome boost for the visitors ahead of the international break while extending their unbeaten run in the Bundesliga.

Mainz, meanwhile, will have two weeks to regroup and address their recent struggles as they look to get their league campaign back on track.

#2774. Key takeaways from Washington Commanders loss to Rams in NFL Week 9

The Commanders entered Week 9 officially in now-or-never mode. Washington had but two wins entering Sunday's game against the Los Angeles Rams.

They were still in the mix despite an unreal rash of injuries to star players, due to a poor-playing NFC East.

They needed to start stacking wins, especially with a massive upcoming clash with the New York Giants next Thursday night.

That meant beating the super-talented (and also injury-plagued) Rams.

The Commanders had a shot to win at the end but came up just short in a 20-13 loss to the Rams at Northwest Stadium.

They have fallen to 2-5-1 and the New York Giants beat Philadelphia to improve to 5-2-1, putting the conversation about Washington competing for the division title to rest. Their season could essentially be over with a Thursday night loss to the Giants.

There's plenty of time to talk about that.

Let's examine some key takeaways from Sunday's clash with the Rams after watching it live:

Washington entered Sunday's fourth quarter all tied up with the Rams. That game status didn't last long. Puka Nakua – the receiver was a Commanders killer – made a leaping touchdown catch to give Los Angeles its largest lead of the day, however slim it may have been.

It was a big moment for the Commanders to respond. They weren't able to do that, coming up short on third down and then passed on a 58-yard field goal attempt for a punt.

The long ensuing Rams started working the clock against Washington as the clock dipped below six minutes. The Commanders didn't have momentum on their side. They scored 10 points in the first two drives and just three points over the next seven.

Jayden Daniels was able to work the ball into Rams territory, but then Myles Garrett struck. The Rams edge rusher — he had three sacks — flashed past rookie center Matt Gulbin for a huge sack and a 12-yard loss.

Daniels' fourth-down pass was a deep that was a short and allowed former Washington first-round pick Emmanuel Forbes to knock it away.

Washington forced a three-and-out and got the ball back with roughly a minute left. Antonio Williams' fair catch inside the 10-yard line left a long way to go. The distance was cut in half with a 41-yard gain Stefon Diggs. Washington couldn't get much farther and ended failing on that final drive.

Say what you want about the Commanders defense and its' frustrating inconsistency, but coordinator Daronte Jones' offense has been good taking the ball away. The were ranked 10 with 1.4 turnovers created per game.

They did better than that on Sunday against the Rams.

The first one came at the end of the first half, when K'Lavon Chaisson punched the ball out and Nick Cross recovered it.

Cross generated another one in the third quarter, dropping into zone coverage and then leaping to intercept Matthew Stafford's pass.

The bigger is that the Commanders score just three points off those turnovers, which kept this game tight entering the fourth quarter

The Commanders have had serious trouble starting games well. That was the case against the 49ers, where they displayed their worst first-half performance of the season. They also came out flat against the Philadelphia Eagles before picking things up, leading Quinn to highlight his team's quest to start faster and put together its' first complete game of the season.

They got off on the right foot, with Jayden Daniels owning a perfect passer rating into the second quarter while leading two scoring drives. The first was promising but ended in a field goal.

The second was highlighted by a big screen pass to Rachaad White.

Receiver Van Jefferson punched it in against his own team to put Washington ahead.

They should've had a bigger lead, honestly, especially after Chaisson popped the ball free and Cross picked it up.

A Jayden Daniels fumble (on a zone read) two plays later ended a chance to go up two scores. The Commanders gave up another field goal to close out the half (not ideal but better than a touchdown), but still lead 10-6 entering halftime.

#2773. Commanders vs. Rams Week 9 Player of the Game: LB Frankie Luvu

The Washington Commanders came out swinging against the Los Angeles Rams today, but ultimately fell, bringing their record to 2-5-1.

After taking a 10-6 lead into the locker room at halftime, the Commanders struggled to get the offense going. Suddenly, they struggled to sustain drives, penalties became a problem, and the defense spent too much time on the field. They managed a field goal in the third quarter, but the Rams scored 14 points to win the game 20-13.

Despite the defense spending so much time on the field today, veteran linebacker Frankie Luvu understood the assignment. He stepped up in a big way to support a depleted defensive line, and he set the tone for the game, earning Player of the Game honors.

Luvu led the game with 15 tackles (seven solo), one sack, two tackles for loss, and two quarterback hits. He was everywhere he needed to be on the field Sunday, and he is showing the rest of the defense what they should be doing, too. Before this season started, Luvu said that he was focused on putting last year behind him and being a leader on this team.

Today, Luvu stood out as a defensive leader. Knowing how many injuries are plaguing the defensive line, he knew that the linebackers would have to step up. And that's exactly what he did. Sonny Styles and Nick Cross were terrific, too, but Luvu is our Week 9 Player of the Game.

#2771. What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique

What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

They're a team of superstars that don't play superstar basketball.

That's the beauty of this 76ers team. They play a brand of unselfish team-first basketball you rarely see from a team loaded with stars.

LeBron James is one of the two or three greatest players in NBA history, a four-time MVP, a four-time NBA champion, a 1st-ballot Hall of Famer.

Joel Embiid is a seven-time all-star, a former MVP, an Olympic gold medalist and a likely Hall of Famer.

Jaylen Brown is a five-time all-star, an NBA champion, a career 20 points-per-game scorer.

Tyrese Maxey is a two-time all-star, one of only two NBA guards averaging 27.0 points per game over the last four years.

Four certified megastars on one team. And somehow they've figured out how to leave their egos at the front door and play smart, tough, disciplined team basketball, the style of ball you just never see on these superstar-laden teams.

On offense, they patiently work the ball around, always looking for the open man or a positive matchup. You've got this bunch of big-name, high-priced All-Stars who are just as happy to pass the ball as shoot it. That never happens.

The 76ers are sixth in the NBA in assists, and they're one of only four teams with twice as many assists as turnovers. Smart, unselfish, patient, disciplined.

When an all-timer like LeBron averages 7.3 assists per game that speaks volumes. LeBron is fifth in the NBA in assists per game among forwards, which is wild. You can see the joy on his face when one of his passes leads to a basket. When one of the greatest players in history has that mentality, it rubs off on everyone around him.

Embiid hasn't really got going yet on offense and still takes too many 3's, but he's also embraced the team-first mentality, and his 4.8 assists per game are more than one full assist above his career average and 4th-most in the NBA among centers or forward-centers. And still one of the most dominating defensive players in the league.

Brown is averaging 27.0 points, which you expect, but he's also averaging 4.3 assists per game, significantly above his career average of 3.8 coming into the season. He's fit in so well so fast you forget its his first season here.

And what can you even say about Maxey? Just a beast. He's averaging nearly 30 points per game but never wastes a possession, always makes the right decision with the ball in his hands and is still finding time to average 4.3 assists per game.

With James, Embiid, Brown and Maxey, the 76ers are the only NBA team with four guys averaging at least 16 points and 4 assists per game. Only the Pacers, Magic and Raptors have three.

But where we're really seeing this group playing out of their minds is on the defensive end, where this bunch of all-stars, including two newcomers, has been astounding.

The 76ers have the 2nd-best defensive efficiency rating in the league, behind only the Pistons, who they play Wednesday night, and they're allowing the 2nd-fewest points in the league, also behind Detroit. Last year, they allowed 123 points per game through eight games. This year they're at 111.

They're holding teams to 43 percent shooting from the field, 3rd-best in the league and their lowest since 2011-12. Their 9.3 steals per game are 6th-most.

All five starters are among the top 25 players in the league in defensive rating – James is fourth, Embiid 11th, Edgecombe 15th, Maxey 22nd and Brown 23rd. How is that even possible?

It's rare to see superstar-caliber guys play as hard on the defensive end as the offensive end. Everybody on this team embraces it. They hustle, they work, they communicate and most importantly they commit to it.

The biggest concern when the 76ers built this dream team was that it would be a team of individuals out for themselves, but the reality has been the opposite.

In a very short period of time, they've learned how to play together. How to play for each other.

And we haven't even mentioned V.J. Edgecomb, who at 21 years old has figured out how to assimilate into this loaded lineup, never force anything, wait for his opportunities and make huge shots when he gets his turn. Edgecombe is at 12.7 points and 3.0 assists per game, and his numbers are understandably down a bit from last year, but if that bothers him it sure doesn't show.

And the role players – Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Jabari Walker – have each adopted the same ego-free style of basketball, supporting the starters however they need to.

Nick Nurse deserves a ton of credit for getting this bunch to play this brand of hoops. It's not easy to get any NBA player to put team goals ahead of individual goals. But he's got Hall of Famers and all-stars doing it and having a blast along the way. They all seem to love playing basketball the right way and they seem to love playing together.

The 76ers are 7-1 and looking like a real threat to win the East, and they're doing it by playing the game the right way.

By committing to each other and not themselves.

The Denver Broncos just never find a way to make it easy for themselves. They extended their one-score win streak to an NFL record 16 games. However, in true Bo Nix Experience fashion, the team rallied when they needed it most and came out with their fifth-straight win. At 7-2, they head into the bye week needing the rest and in first place in the AFC West.

The Broncos got the ball to start the game and proceeded to dominate the entire quarter with almost 11 minutes of possession.

Bo Nix and the offense would add a field goal on their first drive, then attack down the field on their second. The first big play went to Jaylen Waddle for 22 yards.

Broncos 10, Panthers 0. Full first quarter recap.

Panthers 14, Broncos 13. Full third quarter recap.

A critical play on the drive cam eon third and six where Nix found Courtland Sutton outside for a chunk play to the Panthers' 20 yard line. The Panthers had no quit in this game either. They had to convert on a fourth and 18 on their next drive to keep their hopes alive with three minutes left in the game. Somehow, the Broncos defense always seems to find a way to give up a huge play in impossible down and distances. The ensuing touchdown would narrow the lead to 26-21.

On the ensuing drive, many of us on MHR staff were hoping for a second down play-action type call. Instead we got a pitch outside that Coleman bobbled and fumbled. He was able to recover, but the damage was done and the Broncos would be punting it back to Carolina with plenty of time on the clock for them to execute their own comeback.

The Panthers looked like they would mount a comeback of their own on a 21-yard completion on the first play, but on the second Nik Bonitto batted the ball out of Byrce Young's hand where Denver recovered to ice the game.

#2768. Steelers Alum Jack Ham in Stable Condition After Experiencing Medical Emergency Ahead of Penn State Game Broadcast

Pittsburgh Steelers alum Jack Ham, 77, experienced a medical emergency while he was preparing for Penn State's game broadcast in Seattle on Nov. 7

"It was a gut-wrenching day for Penn State football, with Jack Ham," Nittany Lions coach Matt Campbell said

Ham's broadcast partner, Steve Jones, made a "heroic effort" to assist Ham, and the NFL star was taken to the University of Washington Medical Center

Pittsburgh Steelers great Jack Ham is in stable condition after experiencing a medical emergency while he was preparing for Penn State's game broadcast over the weekend — and the college team is crediting Ham's co-host for helping him make it to the hospital.

After Penn State's 28-25 victory over the University of Washington in Seattle on Saturday, Nov. 7, Nittany Lions coach Matt Campbell revealed that Ham, 77, experienced a medical emergency as he was arriving at Husky Stadium.

Ham's broadcast partner, Steve Jones, then made a "heroic effort" to assist Ham, per ESPN and the Associated Press.

"It was a gut-wrenching day for Penn State football, with Jack Ham. Obviously a really tough situation as we got off the buses here," Campbell said. "Steve Jones and the heroic effort that he did for someone he loves dearly. If not for Steve and what he did, I don't know if we would have as good news as we have right now."

Jack Ham during a game against the Los Angeles Rams at Three Rivers Stadium in Pittsburgh, Pa., on Nov. 29, 1981Credit: George Gojkovich/Getty

"I would also thank the University of Washington, their athletic director, their emergency team," the head coach added, referring to Washington athletic director Patrick Chun and the Huskies' staff. "They were incredible, they were outstanding."

Ham was quickly taken across the street to the University of Washington Medical Center after the emergency, Penn State Athletics told PEOPLE in a statement.

"Penn State Sports Network analyst Jack Ham experienced a medical emergency earlier today and was transported to the University of Washington Medical Center, where he is being treated," an athletics spokesperson wrote. "We do not have further information at this time."

Originally from Johnstown, Pa., Ham has been the radio analyst for Penn State, his alma mater, since 2000. The former linebacker was a six-time All-Pro selection with the Steelers and went on to win four Super Bowls and play in eight Pro Bowl games.

Never miss a story — sign up for PEOPLE's free daily newsletter to stay up-to-date on the best of what PEOPLE has to offer, from celebrity news to compelling human interest stories.

He entered the Pro Football Hall of Fame in 1988 and the College Football Hall of Fame in 1990, and was named to the NFL's 1970s All-Decade Team.

Campbell shared during Saturday's press conference that he told the Penn State players about Ham's status before kickoff.

"The courage of our kids to play with what Jack Ham means to Penn State football, how that guy played the game, how that guy to this day still serves our football program, it was awesome to be able to go win the football game for him and his family," Campbell said, per AP. "Our thoughts and prayers will continue to be with him for a full recovery."

#2767. Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch

Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers and Cavaliers have already played one game that looked like two.

Cleveland led by 29 points during the Sixers' home opener on Oct. 22 and carried a 69-45 advantage into halftime. Philadelphia responded by winning the second half, 69-37, and completing a 114-106 comeback.

It was the Sixers' largest comeback since the NBA began maintaining detailed play-by-play data in 1997-98. Only nine other teams during that span had overcome a deficit of at least 29 points.

Fifteen days later, the teams will meet again Friday night at Rocket Arena. This time, the matchup counts toward the NBA Cup standings.

The 8-1 Sixers have won five consecutive games and opened group play with a 98-93 victory over the Knicks. The 4-4 Cavs have dropped two straight and will make their Cup debut.

Here are five numbers that help frame the rematch.

Philadelphia's comeback required more than a hot shooting stretch.

The Sixers committed 15 turnovers in the first half, allowing Cleveland to build a 22-6 advantage in points off turnovers. Their pressure then changed the game after halftime.

The Cavs committed 21 turnovers by the end of the night. What had been a 16-point Cleveland advantage in points off turnovers finished even at 22 apiece.

"They upped their pressure and physicality," Cavs head coach Kenny Atkinson said after the game. "It was pretty clear. We did not handle it well."

The Sixers scored 64 of their 69 second-half points through their starting lineup. Tyrese Maxey finished with 28 points, and Philadelphia turned a game that appeared finished into a franchise-record comeback.

The first half of the opening meeting now looks like an exception.

The Sixers have committed 12.9 turnovers per 100 possessions this season, the NBA's second-lowest rate. They are also third in turnover differential, averaging 3.4 fewer giveaways than their opponents.

That is notable for a team that entered the season intending to play faster. Philadelphia has pushed the ball without routinely surrendering possessions.

LeBron James' presence as the primary facilitator has helped. So has Maxey's willingness to move the ball when defenses trap him. Joel Embiid has averaged 5.2 assists, third among qualified centers, and the Sixers have increasingly trusted VJ Edgecombe to make decisions late in games.

Cleveland's defense has not forced many mistakes. The Cavs enter Friday near the bottom of the league in opponent turnover rate.

The first meeting swung when the Sixers stopped giving away possessions and forced Cleveland into mistakes of its own.

The Sixers have allowed 105.1 points per 100 possessions, the NBA's second-best defensive rating. Cleveland enters with the league's ninth-ranked offense.

The Cavs are the only top-10 offense the Sixers have faced through their first nine games.

Philadelphia has held opponents to a 50 percent effective field-goal percentage and 31.8 percent shooting from three-point range. Its defensive shot profile has been encouraging, too. Only 46 percent of opposing three-point attempts have been classified as wide open, the fifth-lowest share in the league, and opponents have taken just 22 percent of their threes from the corners.

There is reason for caution. Based on the quality of shots Philadelphia has allowed, only Washington's opponents have underperformed their expected effective field-goal percentage by a wider margin. The Sixers have defended well, but opponents are unlikely to remain this cold indefinitely.

Cleveland, with Donovan Mitchell, James Harden and Evan Mobley, can test whether those numbers hold against high-level individual shot creation.

The Cavs have attempted exactly 42 three-pointers per game and made 37.2 percent of them.

Cleveland went 7 for 29 from beyond the arc in Philadelphia. Mitchell, Harden and De'Andre Hunter combined to shoot 3 for 17. The Cavs scored 60 points in the paint and still reached 106, but their 24.1 percent performance from three prevented them from stopping the Sixers' comeback.

Friday's game could look considerably different if Cleveland generates its normal volume.

The Sixers have controlled the three-point line well so far, but Mitchell is coming off a 27-point performance against Oklahoma City and scored 32 in the first meeting with Philadelphia. Harden added 16 points that night, although his six turnovers became costly as the Sixers increased their pressure.

Philadelphia has been outrebounded by 5.1 per game. The Cavs rank sixth with 47.5 rebounds per contest.

The Sixers have already shown they can survive a major deficit on the glass. They beat Milwaukee by 11 despite getting outrebounded by 22. That is not a formula they will want to test regularly against Cleveland's size.

Mobley had 18 points and eight rebounds in the first meeting. Jarrett Allen added 14 points and seven boards, helping Cleveland win the rebounding battle, 46-44.

Whether Allen plays Friday remains uncertain.

Cleveland listed Allen as questionable because of a right knee injury after he left Wednesday's loss to Oklahoma City in the first quarter. Allen collided knee-to-knee with Thunder forward Chet Holmgren and did not return.

The Cavs also listed Peyton Watson as questionable with right hamstring soreness and Tyrese Proctor as questionable with an ankle injury. Proctor left Wednesday's game during the fourth quarter. Jaylon Tyson is out with a right ankle sprain.

Allen's availability could significantly alter the matchup on the glass. If he sits, Thomas Bryant would likely handle more of the center minutes behind Mobley. If he plays, Cleveland will have the same starting frontcourt that outrebounded the Sixers during the first meeting.

Atkinson has emphasized the possession battle during Cleveland's two-game slide.

"You've got to get more shots than the other team," he said this week. "Rebounding is a big part of it."

That also carries added importance in the NBA Cup, where point differential is the second tiebreaker after head-to-head results.

Philadelphia enters Friday at 1-0 with a plus-five differential in East Group B. Miami is also 1-0 at plus-20, while Cleveland is playing its first group game.

The Sixers already know they can erase 29 points against the Cavs. A cleaner start would be far more useful than proving it twice.

#2766. Falcons take early lead over Bengals in Madrid

The Falcons’ offense made it look easy against the Bengals’ defense on today’s first drive in Madrid.

Bijan Robinson ran the ball three times for 42 yards, Michael Penix completed all three of his passes, and the Bengals committed two penalties on a seven-play, 80-yard drive that ended with a Penix one-yard touchdown pass to Charlie Woerner. It was Woerner’s first career touchdown.

The extra point made it 7-0 Falcons, in a game that’s a big one for both teams, who are in competitive division races.

The Bengals have struggled early in games recently, frequently falling behind in the first quarter and then coming back and winning. They’re going to need to do that again.

#2765. Chiefs fans boo Harrison Butker after first-half miss against Jets

Just before halftime against the New York Jets, the Kansas City Chiefs gained 19 yards over two plays and set up a 56-yard field goal for kicker Harrison Butker with seven seconds remaining. A miss to the right drew groans — but also a flag; a call on the Jets gave Butker another shot at extending the lead, this time from only 51 yards away.

When that kick went right, marking his seventh missed field goal of the season, Chiefs fans made sure their displeasure was heard with thunderous boos.

It's not just the two misses; Butker entered the game with the lowest made-field-goal percentage among qualified, active kickers (only former Los Angeles Rams kicker Harrison Mevis qualified with a lower rate). He is especially inefficient on long-range kicks; he is now 2 for 8 on kicks of 50 or more yards this season.

Arrowhead Stadium's boos represented the feelings of many in Chiefs Kingdom. The team did not trot out Butker for another field-goal attempt on Sunday, though he made all five of his point-after attempts.

Late in the game, CBS sideline reporter Tracy Wolfson pointed to the obvious: the team needs to seriously consider making the change and signing Justin Tucker to the active roster in Week 10.

She said special-teams coordinator Dave Toub was hopeful Butker would go 100% against the Jets, so a tough conversation may be coming soon between the coaches and Kansas City's long-time kicker.

#2764. James Pearce Jr. is active for the Falcons

The eight-game suspension of Falcons defensive end James Pearce Jr. has ended. His second NFL season officially starts on Sunday in Madrid.

Pearce is active for the Week 9 game against the Bengals.

Pearce will play despite back tightness that made him a limited participant in Friday’s practice.

The inactives for the Falcons on Sunday include quarterback Andy Dalton (emergency QB), quarterback Jack Strand, receiver Zachariah Branch, cornerback Billy Bowman Jr., cornerback Robert Longerbeam, and defensive end Drake Jackson.

Bowman, who missed the last two games with a hamstring, was considered to be healthy and ready to return.

Also out are running back Brian Robinson Jr. and defensive end Samson Ekuban. Both were placed on injured reserve recently, taking them off the 53-man roster.

A first-round pick in 2025, Pearce had 10.5 sacks as a rookie, and he finished third in the NFL Defensive Rookie of the Year voting. Felony charges filed after he repeatedly rammed his ex-girlfriend’s car and resisted arrest triggered the eight-game suspension.

#2763. Houston Texans’ Pro Bowler Drawing Trade Interest From Multiple Teams

The NFL's trade deadline arrives on Tuesday, and right now, the Houston Texans seem more poised to be sellers rather than buyers. Yet, depending on the outcome of Sunday's games, there's still a chance the Texans could either stand pat or possibly even look to upgrade the roster.

Yet, for now, teams have been calling about one of their three-time Pro Bowlers.

Sep 13, 2026; Houston, Texas, USA; Houston Texans defensive end Jadeveon Clowney (90) after a play during the game against the Buffalo Bills at Reliant Stadium. Mandatory Credit: Troy Taormina-Imagn Images

The Texans haven't quite lived up to their potential in 2026. Yet, after winning two of four matchups in October, Houston has been trending in the right direction. That hasn't prevented teams from trying to trade for some of their players.

According to NFL Network insider Ian Rapoport, multiple teams have reached out about trading for three-time Pro Bowler Jadeveon Clowney.

"Jadeveon Clowney, one of the players that teams have checked in on ahead of the trade deadline to see if he would be available. Our colleague, Adam Schefter of ESPN, reported that. "

Clowney returned to Houston with a one-year contract in free agency this past offseason, and he's tallied 1.5 sacks in five games (zero starts) this season.
